Requires the `mcp` package, version 2.x.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport asyncio\nimport concurrent.futures\nimport contextlib\nimport json\nimport threading\nfrom typing import Any, Dict, Iterator, List\n\nfrom mcp import Client\n\n\nclass RemoteToolError(ValueError):\n    \"\"\"The server rejected a call. A ValueError, like the simulator's own rejections.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass McpTools:\n    \"\"\"The five public methods, each a blocking MCP tool call.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, client: Client, loop: asyncio.AbstractEventLoop) -> None:\n        self._client = client\n        self._loop = loop\n\n    def _call(self, tool: str, arguments: Dict[str, Any]) -> Any:\n        future = asyncio.run_coroutine_threadsafe(self._client.call_tool(tool, arguments), self._loop)\n        result = future.result()\n        text = \"\".join(getattr(block, \"text\", \"\") for block in result.content)\n        if result.is_error:\n            raise RemoteToolError(text)\n        return json.loads(text)\n\n    def rules(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n        return self._call(\"rules\", {})\n\n    def map(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n        return self._call(\"map\", {})\n\n    def telemetry_log(self) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:\n        return self._call(\"telemetry_log\", {})\n\n    def uplink(self, commands: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:\n        return self._call(\"uplink\", {\"commands\": commands})\n\n    def advance(self, ticks: int = 1) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n        return self._call(\"advance\", {\"ticks\": ticks})\n\n    def tool_names(self) -> List[str]:\n        future = asyncio.run_coroutine_threadsafe(self._client.list_tools(), self._loop)\n        return [tool.name for tool in future.result().tools]\n\n\n@contextlib.contextmanager\ndef connect(target: Any) -> Iterator[McpTools]:\n    \"\"\"Open an MCP session on a background event loop and yield blocking tools.\"\"\"\n    loop = asyncio.new_event_loop()\n    thread = threading.Thread(target=loop.run_forever, name=\"lightlag-mcp-client\", daemon=True)\n    thread.start()\n    ready: \"concurrent.futures.Future[Client]\" = concurrent.futures.Future()\n    stop_holder: List[asyncio.Event] = []\n\n    async def session() -> None:\n        # The simulator\n4: runs a drive through `drive()` with the hidden values; a controller can run the same function\n5: with its own estimates, so nobody has to guess how the published formulas are applied.\n6: Standard library only, Python 3.9+.\n7: \"\"\"\n8: \n9: from __future__ import annotations\n10: \n11: import math\n12: from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Sequence, Tuple\n13: \n14: TERRAIN_CODES = {\"b\": \"bedrock\", \"r\": \"regolith\", \"s\": \"sand\", \"k\": \"rocks\"}\n15: \n16: # Published physics. `rules()` returns this table unchanged.\n17: PHYSICS: Dict[str, Any] = {\n18:     \"segment_m\": 0.25,                  # a drive is integrated in steps of this many wheel metres\n19:     \"max_tilt\": 0.36,                   # rise over run (about 20 degrees); steeper ground stops the rover\n20:     \"slip_base_ranges\": {\"bedrock\": [0.01, 0.05], \"regolith\": [0.05, 0.12],\n21:                          \"sand\": [0.15, 0.35], \"rocks\": [0.12, 0.24]},\n22:     \"slip_grade_gain\": {\"bedrock\": 0.8, \"regolith\": 1.5, \"sand\": 3.5, \"rocks\": 1.2},\n23:     \"slip_noise\": 0.02,                 # one uniform draw in +-this per executed drive\n24:     \"slip_max\": 0.95,\n25:     \"side_slip_gain\": {\"bedrock\": 0.1, \"regolith\": 0.3, \"sand\": 0.8, \"rocks\": 0.2},\n26:     \"drive_cost_per_wheel_m\": {\"bedrock\": 0.30, \"regolith\": 0.36, \"sand\": 0.50, \"rocks\": 0.60},\n27:     \"climb_cost_per_m\": 2.0,            # energy per metre of height gained; descending returns nothing\n28: }\n29: \n30: \n31: class Surface:\n32:     \"\"\"A height grid with bilinear interpolation, and one terrain class per grid cell.\n33: \n34:     `elevation_cm[j][i]` is the height in centimetres at x = i * cell, y = j * cell.\n35:     `terrain[j][i]` is the class code of the cell with that vertex as its south-west corner.\n36:     \"\"\"\n37: \n38:     def __init__(self, cell: float, elevation_cm: Sequence[Sequence[int]], terrain: Sequence[str]) -> None:\n39:         self.cell = float(cell)\n40:         self.h: List[List[float]] = [[value / 100.0 for value in row] for row in elevation_cm]\n41:         self.ny, self.nx = len(self.h), len(self.h[0])\n42:         if self.ny < 2 or self.nx < 2 or any(len(row) != self.nx for row in self.h):\n43:             raise ValueError(\"the elevation grid must be rectangular, at least 2 by 2\")\n44:         if len(terrain) != self.ny - 1 or any(len(row) != self.nx - 1 for row in terrain):\n45:             raise ValueError(\"terrain needs one code per grid cell\")\n46:         self.codes: List[str] = [str(row) for row in terrain]\n47:         self.width = (self.nx - 1) * self.cell\n48:         self.height = (self.ny - 1) * self.cell\n49: \n50:     def inside(self, x: float, y: float) -> bool:\n51:         return 0.0 <= x <= self.width and 0.0 <= y <= self.height\n52: \n53:     def locate(self, x: float, y: float) -> Tuple[int, int, float, float]:\n54:         \"\"\"The cell (i, j) holding the point and the point's fractions inside it.\"\"\"\n55:         fx, fy = x / self.cell, y / self.cell\n56:         i = min(self.nx - 2, max(0, int(fx)))\n57:         j = min(self.ny - 2, max(0, int(fy)))\n58:         return i, j, fx - i, fy - j\n59: \n60:     def sample(self, x: float, y: float) -> Tuple[float, float, float, str]:\n61:         \"\"\"Height, d(height)/dx, d(height)/dy and terrain code at a point.\"\"\"\n62:         cell = self.cell\n63:         fx, fy = x / cell, y / cell\n64:         i = min(self.nx - 2, max(0, int(fx)))\n65:         j = min(self.ny - 2, max(0, int(fy)))\n66:         tx, ty = fx - i, fy - j\n67:         low, high = self.h[j], self.h[j + 1]\n68:         h00, h10, h01, h11 = low[i], low[i + 1], high[i], high[i + 1]\n69:         height = (h00 * (1 - tx) + h10 * tx) * (1 - ty) + (h01 * (1 - tx) + h11 * tx) * ty\n70:         gx = ((h10 - h00) * (1 - ty) + (h11 - h01) * ty) / cell\n71:         gy = ((h01 - h00) * (1 - tx) + (h11 - h10) * tx) / cell\n72:         return height, gx, gy, self.codes[j][i]\n73: \n74:     def elevation(self, x: float, y: float) -> float:\n75:         return self.sample(x, y)[0]\n76: \n77:     def slope(self, x: float, y: float) -> float:\n78:         _, gx, gy, _ = self.sample(x, y)\n79:         return math.hypot(gx, gy)\n80: \n81:     def terrain(self, x: float, y: float) -> str:\n82:         return TERRAIN_CODES[self.sample(x, y)[3]]\n83: \n84:     def cell_max_slope(self, i: int, j: int) -> float:\n85:         \"\"\"The steepest slope anywhere in cell (i, j). On a bilinear patch that is at a corner.\"\"\"\n86:         low, high, cell = self.h[j], self.h[j + 1], self.cell\n87:         h00, h10, h01, h11 = low[i], low[i + 1], high[i], high[i + 1]\n88:         south, north = (h10 - h00) / cell, (h11 - h01) / cell\n89:         west, east = (h01 - h00) / cell, (h11 - h10) / cell\n90:         return max(math.hypot(south, west), math.hypot(south, east),\n91:                    math.hypot(north, west), math.hypot(north, east))\n92: \n93: \n94: def drive(surface: Surface, physics: Dict[str, Any], x: float, y: float, heading_deg: float, metres: float,\n95:           slip_base: Dict[str, float], noise: float = 0.0, battery: Optional[float] = None) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n96:     \"\"\"One drive command: `metres` of wheel travel at `heading_deg` from (x, y).\n97: \n98:     Per step of `segment_m` wheel metres, with the ground sampled where the step starts:\n99:       grade  = slope along the heading (rise over run), cross = slope to the left of it\n100:       slip   = clip(slip_base[terrain] + slip_grade_gain[terrain] * max(0, grade) + noise, 0, slip_max)\n101:       the rover moves segment * (1 - slip) along the heading and\n102:       segment * side_slip_gain[terrain] * cross to the right (downhill across the heading)\n103:       energy = segment * drive_cost_per_wheel_m[terrain] + climb_cost_per_m * max(0, height gained)\n104:     The step is refused, and the drive ends, if it would leave the map (`stop` = \"map_boundary\")\n105:     or end on ground steeper than `max_tilt` (`stop` = \"tilt\").     # a drive is integrated in steps of this many wheel metres\n    \"max_tilt\": 0.36,                   # rise over run (about 20 degrees); steeper ground stops the rover\n    \"slip_base_ranges\": # energy per metre of height gained; descending returns nothing\n}\n\n\nclass Surface:\n    \"\"\"A height grid with bilinear interpolation, and one terrain class per grid cell.\n\n    `elevation_cm[j][i]` is the height in centimetres at x = i * cell, y = j * cell.\n    `terrain[j][i]` is the class code of the cell with that vertex as its south-west corner.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, cell: float, elevation_cm: Sequence[Sequence[int]], terrain: Sequence[str]) -> None:\n        self.cell = float(cell)\n        self.h: List[List[float]] = [[value / 100.0 for value in row] for row in elevation_cm]\n        self.ny, self.nx = len(self.h), len(self.h[0])\n        if self.ny < 2 or self.nx < 2 or any(len(row) != self.nx for row in self.h):\n            raise ValueError(\"the elevation grid must be rectangular, at least 2 by 2\")\n        if len(terrain) != self.ny - 1 or any(len(row) != self.nx - 1 for row in terrain):\n            raise float, float]:\n        \"\"\"The cell (i, j) holding the point and the point's fractions inside it.\"\"\"\n        fx, fy = x / self.cell, y / self.cell\n        i = min(self.nx - 2, max(0, d(height)/dx, d(height)/dy and terrain code at a point.\"\"\"\n        cell = self.cell\n        fx, fy = x / cell, y / cell\n        i = min(self.nx - 2, max(0, int(fx)))\n        j = min(self.ny - Stop the program with `kill <pid>`.\n\nWhy this exists. An agent's shell tool runs one command at a time under a time limit. Such a\ntool typically waits until everything the command started has let go of its output, and when\nthe limit is reached it stops the whole process group. A program started with `&` or `nohup`\nis still in that group, and in a form like `cd /app && nohup python3 autopilot.py > log 2>&1 &`\nthe shell that waits for it still holds the tool's output. The call then hangs until the limit\nand the program is killed with it, in the middle of a campaign that does not wait.\n\nA program started here has its own session and no link to the caller's input or output: it\nreads from nowhere and writes to the log file, unbuffered so the log is current. return its process id.\"\"\"\n    environment = dict(os.environ, PYTHONUNBUFFERED=\"1\")\n    with open(log, \"ab\") as stream:\n        process = subprocess.Popen(list(command), stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL, stdout=stream,\n                                   stderr=subprocess.STDOUT, start_new_session=True,\n                                   close_fds=True, env=environment)\n    return process.pid\n\n\ndef main(argv: Optional[Sequence[str]] = None) -> int:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(\n        description=\"Start a program that keeps running after this command returns.\",\n        usage=\"python3 detach.py [--log FILE] command [argument ...]\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--log\", default=\"autopilot.log\", help=\"file the program's output is appended to\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"command\", nargs=argparse.REMAINDER, help=\"the program and its arguments\")\n    args = parser.parse_args(argv)\n    command = args.command[1:] if args.command[:1] == [\"--\"] else args.command\n    if not command:\n        parser.error(\"give the program to start, for example: python3 autopilot.py\")\n    try:\n        pid = start(command, args.log)\n    except OSError as failure:\n        print(\"could not start {}: {}\".format(command[0], failure), file=sys.stderr)\n        return 1\n    print(\"started pid {};
